=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given sequences of length N: A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) and B = (B_1, B_2, \dots, B_N).
Let S be a subset of \lbrace1, 2, \dots, N\rbrace of size K.
Here, find the minimum possible value of the following expression:

\displaystyle \left(\max_{i \in S} A_i\right) \times \left(\sum_{i \in S} B_i\right).

You are given T test cases; solve each of them.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format. Here, \mathrm{case}_i denotes the i-th test case.
T
\mathrm{case}_1
\mathrm{case}_2
\vdots
\mathrm{case}_T

Each test case is given in the following format:
N K
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N
B_1 B_2 \dots B_N

Output

Print T lines. The i-th line should contain the answer for the i-th test case.

Constraints


- 1 \leq T \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq K \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq A_i, B_i \leq 10^6
- The sum of N over all test cases is at most 2 \times 10^5.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
3 2
3 7 6
9 2 4
5 3
6 4 1 5 9
8 6 5 1 7
10 6
61 95 61 57 69 49 46 47 14 43
39 79 48 92 90 76 30 16 30 94

Sample Output 1

42
60
14579

In the first test case, for S = \{2, 3\}, the value of the expression is 7 \times (2 + 4) = 42, which is the minimum.

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

heap=[] # min heap of -B
sum_selected=0
for group:
 for a,b in group:
   if len(heap)<K:
     heapq.heappush(heap,-b)
     sum_selected += b
   else:

     if b < max_b:
       old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
       sum_selected += b - old

Implementation details:
- Read all ints via sys.stdin.buffer.read().split() for speed.
- T first.
- For each test case:
  N=int, K=int
  A list length N, B list length N
  items = list(zip(A,B))
  items.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
  heap=[]
  s=0
  ans=10**30 maybe 10**30 enough? Max product: maxA 1e6 * sumB up to K*1e6 = 2e11 => 2e17. 10**30 safe.
  i=0
  while i<N:
    a=items[i][0]
    while i<N and items[i][0]==a:
       b=items[i][1]
       if len(heap)<K:
          heapq.heappush(heap,-b); s+=b
       else:
          if b < -heap[0]:
             old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
             s += b - old
       i+=1
    if len(heap)==K:
       val=a*s
       if val<ans: ans=val
  output ans

import sys, heapq
def main():
 data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
 it=iter(data)
 T=next(it)
 out=[]
 for _ in range(T):
   N=next(it); K=next(it)
   A=[next(it) for _ in range(N)]
   B=[next(it) for _ in range(N)]
   items=sorted(zip(A,B), key=lambda x:x[0])
   heap=[]
   s=0
   ans=10**30
   i=0
   while i<N:
     a=items[i][0]
     while i<N and items[i][0]==a:
       b=items[i][1]
       if len(heap)<K:
          heapq.heappush(heap, -b)
          s += b
       else:
          max_b = -heap[0]
          if b < max_b:
             old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
             s += b - old
       i += 1
     if len(heap)==K:
       val = a * s
       if val < ans: ans=val
   out.append(str(ans))
 sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
if __name__=="__main__": main()

Now, let's revisit the proof of min f = OPT because it's central. Let's formalize:
For any x, let P_x = {i | A_i <= x}, g(x)=sum of K smallest B_i in P_x if |P_x|>=K. Let f(x)=x*g(x). For any feasible S, let x=max A_i in S. Then S subset P_x, so g(x) <= sum_{i in S}B_i. Thus f(x) <= x*sum = value(S). Therefore min_x f(x) <= OPT. Conversely, for any x, let S_x be K items in P_x with smallest B. Then maxA(S_x) <= x, so value(S_x)=maxA(S_x)*g(x) <= x*g(x)=f(x). Hence OPT <= value(S_x) <= f(x) for all x. Thus OPT <= min_x f(x). So equality. This proof doesn't require S_x feasible? It is feasible size K. Good.

Thus algorithm.

Now, let's think about final answer format. The user says "Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block." We should output a code block with Python code. No extra text. The final answer can be:

```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    out = []
    for _ in range(t):
        N = data[idx]; K = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        A = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
        B = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
        items = sorted(zip(A, B), key=lambda x: x[0])
        heap = []
        s = 0
        ans = 10**30
        i = 0
        while i < N:
            a = items[i][0]
            while i < N and items[i][0] == a:
                b = items[i][1]
                if len(heap) < K:
                    heapq.heappush(heap, -b)
                    s += b
                else:
                    max_b = -heap[0]
                    if b < max_b:
                        old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
                        s += b - old
                i += 1
            if len(heap) == K:
                val = a * s
                if val < ans:
                    ans = val
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's maybe use `readline` to be memory safe and code simple:
def main():
    import sys, heapq
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    T = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N, K = map(int, input().split())
        A = list(map(int, input().split()))
        B = list(map(int, input().split()))
        items = sorted(zip(A, B), key=lambda x: x[0])
        heap = []
        s = 0
        ans = 10**30
        i = 0
        while i < N:
            a = items[i][0]
            while i < N and items[i][0] == a:
                b = items[i][1]
                if len(heap) < K:
                    heapq.heappush(heap, -b)
                    s += b
                else:
                    max_b = -heap[0]
                    if b < max_b:
                        old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
                        s += b - old
                i += 1
            if len(heap) == K:
                val = a * s
                if val < ans:
                    ans = val
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))
This is fine. But if there are blank lines in input, `int(input())` would fail. Input standard no blank. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden "A_i, B_i" values maybe not fit in C int? Python int. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possible problem with "heap" and "s" if we use `heapq.heapreplace` and then `s += b - old`; if old is max_b and b<old, s decreases. If old is not in heap? It was
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
import heapq

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    out = []

    for _ in range(t):
        N = data[idx]
        K = data[idx + 1]
        idx += 2

        A = data[idx:idx + N]
        idx += N
        B = data[idx:idx + N]
        idx += N

        items = sorted(zip(A, B), key=lambda x: x[0])

        heap = []          # min-heap of -B, so heap[0] is the largest selected B
        sum_selected = 0
        ans = 10**30

        i = 0
        while i < N:
            a = items[i][0]

            while i < N and items[i][0] == a:
                b = items[i][1]

                if len(heap) < K:
                    heapq.heappush(heap, -b)
                    sum_selected += b
                else:
                    max_b = -heap[0]
                    if b < max_b:
                        old = -heapq.heapreplace(heap, -b)
                        sum_selected += b - old

                i += 1

            if len(heap) == K:
                ans = min(ans, a * sum_selected)

        out.append(str(ans))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
